In a decade of a lot of soul music with bravado and show, <a href=\\\"https://www.tracklib.com/artist/isaac-hayes/\\\">Isaac Hayes</a> took a more introspective and deeper approach with 1969’s <i>Hot Buttered Soul</i>, which marked his commercial breakthrough on Stax Records. And it wasn’t just a personal breakthrough: it became a landmark in soul music. The album merged R&amp;B/soul with orchestral structures and a psychedelic sound, a newer and much funkier sound by the original <i>baldhead slick</i>. With himself pictured on the album cover wearing sunglasses and a gold chain—going against the era’s dress code of sharp Motown suits.</p><p>And that classic album is only the tip of the iceberg of how Isaac Hayes reinvented soul music. Take &#x27;Theme from Shaft&#x27;, which made him the first African American artist to win an Academy Award for a soundtrack, contributing to the birth of the Blaxploitation genre. And then we’re not even talking yet about <i>Black Moses</i>, <i>The Isaac Hayes Movement</i>, and the rest of his vast iconic catalog...</p>\",\"id\":\"2f8ada56-f1ed-4b2a-89a2-2a2170e78dde\"},{\"type\":\"track\",\"value\":{\"hide_it\":false,\"inline_songs\":[91306,91344],\"compact\":false,\"stripped_mode\":false,\"showCount\":false},\"id\":\"3331a67a-8fae-40f4-80b0-8071d2b1a848\"},{\"type\":\"paragraph\",\"value\":\"<p>Over a thousand tracks by hip-hop artists like The Notorious B.I.G. Diddy and Alicia Keys who have also sampled his work.”</p>\",\"id\":\"080e77a1-0f6b-4778-98d7-9893d59f409e\"},{\"type\":\"track\",\"value\":{\"hide_it\":false,\"inline_songs\":[91457],\"compact\":false,\"stripped_mode\":false,\"showCount\":false},\"id\":\"b583f925-1479-4769-ad65-73488b2e0fd2\"},{\"type\":\"article_quote\",\"value\":{\"text\":\"\\\"It’s mind-blowing to realize that this is music he made in an era without digital recording. Cole’s <i>Middle Child</i></a> was a great example to me: over 45 years later, J. Cole brought back the music of First Choice, breathing new life and revenue into their music. To me, that’s ground-breaking. And for producers, it brings back the experience of digging in the crates in a contemporary—and safe and legal—way.
